style="font-weight:normal"><i><a href="#Family">more...</a></i></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Irene_of_Hungary" title="Irene of Hungary">Empress Irene of Byzantium</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">Dynasty</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_dynasty" title="Árpád dynasty">Árpád dynasty</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Father</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_I_of_Hungary" title="Béla I of Hungary">Béla I of Hungary</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Mother</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Richeza_of_Poland,_Queen_of_Hungary" title="Richeza of Poland, Queen of Hungary">Richeza or Adelaide of Poland</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Ladislaus I</b> (<a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">I. László</i>, <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a>: <i lang="hr">Ladislav I.</i>, <a href="/wiki/Slovak_language" title="Slovak language">Slovak</a>: <i lang="sk">Ladislav I.</i>, <a href="/wiki/Polish_language" title="Polish language">Polish</a>: <i lang="pl">Władysław I</i>; <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 1040 – 29 July 1095), also known as <b>Saint Ladislas</b>, was <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a> from 1077 and <a href="/wiki/King_of_Croatia" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Croatia">King of Croatia</a> from 1091. He was the second son of King <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_I_of_Hungary" title="Béla I of Hungary">Béla I of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Richeza_of_Poland,_Queen_of_Hungary" title="Richeza of Poland, Queen of Hungary">Richeza (or Adelaide) of Poland</a>. After Béla's death in 1063, Ladislaus and his elder brother, <a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9za_I_of_Hungary" title="Géza I of Hungary">Géza</a>, acknowledged their cousin <a href="/wiki/Solomon_of_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Solomon of Hungary">Solomon</a> as the lawful king in exchange for receiving their father's former <a href="/wiki/Duchy_(Kingdom_of_Hungary)" title="Duchy (Kingdom of Hungary)">duchy</a>, which included one-third of the kingdom. They cooperated with Solomon for the next decade. Ladislaus's <a href="/wiki/Saint_Ladislaus_legend" title="Saint Ladislaus legend">most popular legend</a>, which narrates his fight with a "Cuman" (a <a href="/wiki/Turkic_peoples" title="Turkic peoples">Turkic</a> nomad marauder) who abducted a Hungarian girl, is connected to this period. The brothers' relationship with Solomon deteriorated in the early 1070s, and they rebelled against him. Géza was proclaimed king in 1074, but Solomon maintained control of the western regions of his kingdom. During Géza's reign, Ladislaus was his brother's most influential adviser.
</p><p>Géza died in 1077, and his supporters made Ladislaus king. Solomon resisted Ladislaus with assistance from King <a href="/wiki/Henry_IV_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry IV of Germany">Henry IV of Germany</a>. Ladislaus supported Henry IV's opponents during the <a href="/wiki/Investiture_Controversy" title="Investiture Controversy">Investiture Controversy</a>. In 1081, Solomon abdicated and acknowledged Ladislaus's reign, but he conspired to regain the royal crown, and Ladislaus imprisoned him. Ladislaus <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> the first Hungarian saints (including his distant relatives, King <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">Stephen I</a> and Duke <a href="/wiki/Saint_Emeric_of_Hungary" title="Saint Emeric of Hungary">Emeric</a>) in 1085. He set Solomon free during the canonization ceremony.
</p><p>After a <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1000%E2%80%931301)#Pagan_revolts,_wars_and_consolidation_(1038–1116)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1000–1301)">series of civil wars</a>, Ladislaus's main focus was the restoration of public safety. He introduced severe legislation, punishing those who violated property rights with death or mutilation. He occupied almost all <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_(925%E2%80%931102)" title="Kingdom of Croatia (925–1102)">Croatia</a> in 1091, which marked the beginning of an expansion period for the medieval <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a>. Ladislaus's victories over the <a href="/wiki/Pechenegs" title="Pechenegs">Pechenegs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cumans" title="Cumans">Cumans</a> ensured the security of his kingdom's eastern borders for about 150 years. His relationship with the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> deteriorated during the last years of his reign, as the popes claimed that Croatia was their <a href="/wiki/Fief" title="Fief">fief</a>, but Ladislaus denied their claims.
</p><p>Ladislaus was canonized on 27 June 1192 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Celestine_III" title="Pope Celestine III">Pope Celestine III</a>. Legends depict him as a pious knight-king, "the incarnation of the late-medieval Hungarian ideal of chivalry."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002187_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002187-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> He is a popular saint in Hungary and neighboring nations, where many churches are dedicated to him.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span id="Early_years_.28before_1064.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years_(before_1064)">Early years (before 1064)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early years (before 1064)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Ladislaus was the second son of the future King <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_I_of_Hungary" title="Béla I of Hungary">Béla I of Hungary</a> and his wife, <a href="/wiki/Richeza_of_Poland,_Queen_of_Hungary" title="Richeza of Poland, Queen of Hungary">Richeza (or Adelaide)</a>, who was a daughter of King <a href="/wiki/Mieszko_II_Lambert" title="Mieszko II Lambert">Mieszko II Lambert of Poland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199678,_107_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199678,_107-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus and his elder brother, <a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9za_I_of_Hungary" title="Géza I of Hungary">Géza</a>, were born in <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a>, where Béla had settled in the 1030s after being <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_I_of_Hungary#In_exile_(1031–c._1048)" title="Béla I of Hungary">banished from Hungary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus was born around 1040.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus's "physical and spiritual makeup testified to God's gracious will even at his birth", according to his late-12th-century <i>Legend</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002174_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002174-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The almost contemporaneous <a href="/wiki/Gallus_Anonymus" title="Gallus Anonymus">Gallus Anonymus</a> wrote that Ladislaus was "raised from childhood in Poland" and almost became a "<a href="/wiki/Polish_people" title="Polish people">Pole</a> in his ways and life".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Gallus_27_97_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gallus_27_97-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He received a <a href="/wiki/Slavic_languages" title="Slavic languages">Slavic</a> name: "Ladislaus" is derived from "<a href="/wiki/Vladislav" title="Vladislav">Vladislav</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Béla and his family returned to Hungary around 1048.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Béla received the so-called "<a href="/wiki/Duchy_(Kingdom_of_Hungary)" title="Duchy (Kingdom of Hungary)">Duchy</a>"&#160;&#8211;&#32;which encompassed one-third of the kingdom&#160;&#8211;&#32;from his brother, King <a href="/wiki/Andrew_I_of_Hungary" title="Andrew I of Hungary">Andrew I of Hungary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199960_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199960-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200130_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200130-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199679_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199679-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The <i><a href="/wiki/Illuminated_Chronicle" class="mw-redirect" title="Illuminated Chronicle">Illuminated Chronicle</a></i> mentions that Andrew's son, <a href="/wiki/Solomon_of_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Solomon of Hungary">Solomon</a>, "was <a href="/wiki/Anointing" title="Anointing">anointed</a> king with the consent of Duke Bela and his sons Geysa and Ladislaus"<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> in 1057 or 1058.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Béla, who had been Andrew's heir before Solomon's coronation, left for Poland in 1059; his sons accompanied him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> They returned with Polish reinforcements and began a rebellion against Andrew.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199960_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199960-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200131_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200131-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> After defeating Andrew, Béla was crowned king on 6 December 1060.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200131_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200131-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Solomon left the country, taking refuge in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199961_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199961-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERobinson199953_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERobinson199953-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Béla I died on 11 September 1063, some time before German troops <a href="/wiki/German_invasion_of_Hungary_(1063)" title="German invasion of Hungary (1063)">entered Hungary</a> in order to restore Solomon.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus and his brothers, Géza and <a href="/wiki/Lampert_of_Hungary" title="Lampert of Hungary">Lampert</a>, went back to Poland, and Solomon was once again crowned king in <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kesfeh%C3%A9rv%C3%A1r" title="Székesfehérvár">Székesfehérvár</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107_4-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188–89_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198188–89-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The three brothers returned when the Germans left Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200226–27_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200226–27-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> To avoid another civil war, the brothers signed a treaty with Solomon on 20 January 1064,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200226–27_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200226–27-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198189_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198189-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> acknowledging Solomon's reign in exchange for their father's duchy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198189_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198189-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Duke_in_Hungary_.281064.E2.80.931077.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Duke_in_Hungary_(1064–1077)">Duke in Hungary (1064–1077)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Duke in Hungary (1064–1077)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Ladislaus and Géza probably divided the administration of their duchy; Ladislaus seems to have received the regions around <a href="/wiki/Biharia" title="Biharia">Bihar</a> (now Biharia, Romania).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201127_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201127-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996106_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996106-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Géza and Ladislaus cooperated with King Solomon between 1064 and 1071.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996106_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996106-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> The most popular story in Ladislaus's later legends&#160;&#8211;&#32;his fight with a "<a href="/wiki/Turkic_peoples" title="Turkic peoples">Cuman</a>" warrior who abducted a Christian maiden&#160;&#8211;&#32;occurred during this period.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002176–177_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002176–177-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107–108_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996107–108-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The relationship between the king and his cousins became tense in the early 1070s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> When Géza accompanied Solomon on a military campaign against the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantine Empire</a> in 1072, Ladislaus stayed behind with half of the ducal troops in <a href="/wiki/Ny%C3%ADrs%C3%A9g" class="mw-redirect" title="Nyírség">Nyírség</a> to "avenge his brother with a strong hand"<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> if Solomon harmed Géza.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_(4).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg/220px-K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg/330px-K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg/440px-K%C3%A9pes_kr%C3%B3nika_-_72.oldal_-_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_herceg_birokra_k%C3%A9l_a_le%C3%A1nyrabl%C3%B3_kunnal_%284%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="682" /></a><figcaption>After the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Kerl%C3%A9s" title="Battle of Kerlés">Battle of Kerlés</a> in 1068, Saint Ladislaus is fighting a duel with a <a href="/wiki/Cumans" title="Cumans">Cuman</a> warrior who kidnapped a girl (<a href="/wiki/Chronicon_Pictum" title="Chronicon Pictum">Chronicon Pictum</a>, 1358)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Realizing that another civil war was inevitable, the king and dukes launched negotiations to obtain the assistance of foreign powers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> First, Ladislaus visited the <a href="/wiki/Kievan_Rus%27" title="Kievan Rus&#39;">Kievan Rus'</a>, but he returned without reinforcements.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198185-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> He then went to <a href="/wiki/Moravia" title="Moravia">Moravia</a>, and persuaded Duke <a href="/wiki/Otto_I_of_Olomouc" title="Otto I of Olomouc">Otto I of Olomouc</a> to accompany him back to Hungary with <a href="/wiki/Czechs" title="Czechs">Czech</a> troops.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakk1994394-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> By the time they returned to Hungary, the royal army had already invaded the duchy and routed Géza's troops at the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Battle_of_Kemej&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Battle of Kemej (page does not exist)">Battle of Kemej</a> on 26 February 1074.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109_24-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996109-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus met his fleeing brother at <a href="/wiki/V%C3%A1c" title="Vác">Vác</a>, and they decided to continue the fight against Solomon.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> A legend preserved in the <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> mentions that before the battle, Ladislaus "saw in broad daylight a <a href="/wiki/Vision_(spirituality)" title="Vision (spirituality)">vision from heaven</a>" of an angel placing a crown on Géza's head.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Another legendary episode also predicted the dukes' triumph over the king: an "<a href="/wiki/Stoat" title="Stoat">ermine</a> of purest white" jumped from a thorny bush to Ladislaus's lance and then onto his chest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> The decisive <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Mogyor%C3%B3d" title="Battle of Mogyoród">Battle of Mogyoród</a> was fought on 14 March 1074.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus commanded "the troops from <a href="/wiki/Bihar_County" title="Bihar County">Byhor</a>" on the left flank.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128_28-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Solomon was defeated,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198190-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> but instead of surrendering to his cousins, he fled to the western borders of the kingdom to seek assistance from his brother-in-law <a href="/wiki/Henry_IV_of_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry IV of Germany">Henry IV of Germany</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199694_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199694-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Géza was proclaimed king, but Solomon established himself in <a href="/wiki/Mosonmagyar%C3%B3v%C3%A1r" title="Mosonmagyaróvár">Moson</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pressburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Pressburg">Pressburg</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Bratislava,_Slovakia" class="mw-redirect" title="Bratislava, Slovakia">Bratislava, Slovakia</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128_28-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESteinhübel201128-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199694_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk199694-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> During his brother's reign, Ladislaus administered all of their father's former duchy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> He repelled Solomon's attack on <a href="/wiki/Nyitra" class="mw-redirect" title="Nyitra">Nyitra</a> (present-day <a href="/wiki/Nitra,_Slovakia" class="mw-redirect" title="Nitra, Slovakia">Nitra, Slovakia</a>) in August or September 1074, but he could not seize Pressburg.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus was also his brother's main advisor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110_27-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Legend says that Géza decided to build a church dedicated to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Virgin" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Virgin">Holy Virgin</a> in <a href="/wiki/V%C3%A1c" title="Vác">Vác</a> after Ladislaus explained the significance of the wondrous appearance of a <a href="/wiki/Red_deer" title="Red deer">red deer</a> at the place where the church would be erected:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177–178_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002177–178-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>As [King Géza and Duke Ladislaus] were standing at a spot near [Vác], where is now the church of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">blessed apostle Peter</a>, a stag appeared to them with many candles burning upon his horns, and it began to run swifly before them towards the wood, and at the spot where is now the monastery, it halted and stood still. When the soldiers shot their arrows at it, it leapt into the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a>, and they saw it no more. At this sight the blessed Ladislaus said: "Truly that was no stag, but an <a href="/wiki/Angel" title="Angel">angel</a> from God." And King [Géza] said: "Tell me, beloved brother, what may all the candles signify which we saw burning on the stag's horns." The blessed Ladislaus answered: "They are not horns, but wings; they are not burning candles, but shining feathers. It has shown to us that we are to build the church of the Blessed Virgin on the place where it planted its feet, and not elsewhere."</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><i><a href="/wiki/Illuminated_Chronicle" class="mw-redirect" title="Illuminated Chronicle">The Hungarian Illuminated Chronicle</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="His_reign">His reign</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: His reign">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span id="Consolidation_.281077.E2.80.931085.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Consolidation_(1077–1085)">Consolidation (1077–1085)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Consolidation (1077–1085)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg/220px-Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="283" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg/330px-Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg/440px-Laszlo-Coronation-ChroniconPictum.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1720" data-file-height="2210" /></a><figcaption>Angels crowning King Saint Ladislaus (<a href="/wiki/Chronicon_Pictum" title="Chronicon Pictum">Chronicon Pictum</a>, 1358)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Géza I died on 25 April 1077.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200132_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200132-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Since Géza's sons, <a href="/wiki/Coloman,_King_of_Hungary" title="Coloman, King of Hungary">Coloman</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lmos,_Duke_of_Croatia" class="mw-redirect" title="Álmos, Duke of Croatia">Álmos</a>, were <a href="/wiki/Minor_(law)" title="Minor (law)">minors</a>, his supporters proclaimed Ladislaus king instead.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110_27-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996110-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Gallus Anonymus emphasizes that King <a href="/wiki/Boleslaus_II_the_Bold" class="mw-redirect" title="Boleslaus II the Bold">Boleslaus II the Bold</a> of Poland "drove out" Solomon "from Hungary with his forces, and placed [Ladislaus] on the throne"; Boleslaus even called Ladislaus "his king".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012338-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEManteuffel198297_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEManteuffel198297-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Although the <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> emphasizes that Ladislaus "never placed the crown upon his head, for he desired a heavenly crown rather than the earthly crown of a mortal king", all his coins depict him wearing a crown, suggesting that Ladislaus was actually crowned around 1078.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996114_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996114-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Chronicon_93_131_127_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicon_93_131_127-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Shortly after his coronation, Ladislaus promulgated two law books, which incorporated the decisions of an assembly of the "magnates of the kingdom", held in <a href="/wiki/Pannonhalma" title="Pannonhalma">Pannonhalma</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The majority of these laws were draconian measures to defend private property, showing that Ladislaus primarily focused on internal consolidation and security during the first years of his reign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199962_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199962-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Those who were caught <a href="/wiki/Theft" title="Theft">stealing</a> were to be executed, and even criminals who committed minor offenses against property rights were blinded or sold as slaves.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199962_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199962-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> His other laws regulated legal proceedings and economic matters, including the issuing of <a href="/wiki/Summons#Judicial_summons" title="Summons">judicial summons</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Royal_monopoly" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal monopoly">royal monopoly</a> on salt trade.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227_19-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBartlČičajKohútovaLetz200227-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199962_44-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199962-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>If someone, freeman or bondman, should be caught in theft, he shall be <a href="/wiki/Hanging" title="Hanging">hanged</a>. But if he <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary#Human_sanctuary" title="Sanctuary">flees to the church</a> to evade the <a href="/wiki/Gallows" title="Gallows">gallows</a>, he shall be led out of the church and blinded. A bondman caught in theft, if he does not flee to the church, shall be hanged; the owner of the stolen goods shall take a loss in the lost goods. The sons and daughters of a freeman caught in theft who fled to the church, was led out and blinded, if they are ten years old or less, shall retain their freedom; but if they are older than ten years they shall be reduced to servitude and lose all their property. A bondman or freeman who steals a goose or a hen shall lose one eye and shall restore what he has stolen.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><i>Laws of King Ladislas I</i><sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ladislav1_denar1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A small silver coin depicting a cross" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Ladislav1_denar1.jpg/220px-Ladislav1_denar1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/3f/Ladislav1_denar1.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="299" /></a><figcaption>Ladislaus's denar</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> claims that Ladislaus planned to "restore the kingdom" to Solomon and "himself have the dukedom",<sup id="cite_ref-Chronicon_93_131_127_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicon_93_131_127-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996114_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996114-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> but almost all contemporaneous sources contradict this report.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117–118_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117–118-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus approached <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_VII" title="Pope Gregory VII">Pope Gregory VII</a>, who was the primary opponent of Solomon's ally, Henry IV of Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117–118_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117–118-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> At the Pope's request, Ladislaus sheltered <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavarian</a> nobles who had rebelled against Henry.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In 1078 or 1079, Ladislaus married <a href="/wiki/Adelaide_of_Rheinfelden" title="Adelaide of Rheinfelden">Adelaide</a>, a daughter of <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_of_Rheinfelden" title="Rudolf of Rheinfelden">Rudolf of Rheinfelden</a>, whom <a href="/wiki/Saxon_revolt_of_1077%E2%80%931088" title="Saxon revolt of 1077–1088">the German princes had elected</a> to take the place of Henry IV as king.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus supported <a href="/wiki/Leopold_II,_Margrave_of_Austria" title="Leopold II, Margrave of Austria">Leopold II, Margrave of Austria</a>, who also rebelled against Henry IV; however, the German monarch forced Leopold to surrender in May 1078.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERobinson1999191_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERobinson1999191-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Taking advantage of the internal conflicts in the Holy Roman Empire, Ladislaus besieged and captured the fortress of Moson from Solomon in early 1079.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192_50-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> However, Henry IV stormed the western regions of Hungary, and secured Solomon's position.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The German invasion also prevented Ladislaus from assisting Boleslaus the Bold, who fled to Hungary after his subjects expelled him from Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEManteuffel198298_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEManteuffel198298-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus initiated negotiations with Solomon, who abdicated in 1080 or 1081 in exchange for "revenues sufficient to bear the expenses of a king".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192_50-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198192-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119_52-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Chronicon_94_133_128_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicon_94_133_128-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> However, Solomon soon began conspiring against Ladislaus, and Ladislaus imprisoned him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198193-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119_52-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first five Hungarian saints, including the first king of Hungary, <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">Stephen I</a>, and Stephen's son, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Emeric_of_Hungary" title="Saint Emeric of Hungary">Emeric</a>, were <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> during Ladislaus's reign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119_52-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996119-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Stephen's canonization demonstrates Ladislaus's magnanimity, because Ladislaus's grandfather, <a href="/wiki/Vazul" title="Vazul">Vazul</a>, had been blinded on Stephen's orders in the 1030s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199963_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199963-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> Historian László Kontler says that the canonization ceremony, held in August 1083, was also a political act, demonstrating Ladislaus's "commitment to preserving and strengthening" the Christian state.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKontler199964_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKontler199964-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus even dedicated a newly established <a href="/wiki/Benedictine" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedictine">Benedictine</a> monastery&#160;&#8211;&#32;Szentjobb Abbey&#160;&#8211;&#32;to Stephen's right arm, known as the "<a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary#Legacy#His_Holy_Dexter" title="Stephen I of Hungary">Holy Dexter</a>", which was miraculously found intact.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus released Solomon at the time of the ceremony;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> legend said that Stephen's grave could not be opened until he did so.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194_48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik198194-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>[The] Lord, in order to show how merciful [King Stephen I] had been while living in a mortal body, demonstrated his approval of [Stephen's revelation as a saint] before all other works when [the king] was already reigning with Christ to the point that though for three days they struggled with all their might to raise his holy body, it was not by any means to be moved from its place. For in that time, because of the sins, a grave discord arose between the said king Ladislas and his cousin Solomon, because of which, Solomon, captured, was held in prison. Therefore when they tried in vain to raise the body, a certain <a href="/wiki/Recluse" title="Recluse">recluse</a> at the church of the Holy Savior in Bökénysomlyó, by the name of Karitas, whose famous life at the time was held in esteem, confided to the king by a revelation made to her from heaven that they exerted themselves in vain; it would be impossible to transfer the relics of the holy king until unconditional pardon was offered to Solomon, setting him free from the confinement of prison. And thus, bringing him forth from the prison, and repeating the three-day fast, when the third day arrived for the transferal of the holy remains, the stone lying over the grave was lifted up with such ease as if it had been of no weight before.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><i><a href="/wiki/Hartvik" title="Hartvik">Hartvic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Legenda_Hartviciana" title="Legenda Hartviciana">Life of King Stephen of Hungary</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<p>After his release, Solomon made a final effort to regain his crown.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200133_45-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200133-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> He persuaded a <a href="/wiki/Pechenegs" title="Pechenegs">Pecheneg</a> chieftain, Kutesk, to invade Hungary in 1085.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus defeated the invaders at the upper courses of the <a href="/wiki/Tisza_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Tisza River">Tisza River</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193_58-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996121_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996121-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Expansion_.281085.E2.80.931092.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Expansion_(1085–1092)">Expansion (1085–1092)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Expansion (1085–1092)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In August 1087, German princes who opposed Henry IV's rule held a conference in <a href="/wiki/Speyer" title="Speyer">Speyer</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193_58-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEÉrszegiSolymosi198193-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERobinson1999263_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERobinson1999263-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The contemporaneous <a href="/wiki/Bernold_of_St_Blasien" class="mw-redirect" title="Bernold of St Blasien">Bernold of St Blasien</a> mentions that Ladislaus sent envoys to the meeting, and "promised that he would assist [them] with 20,000 knights, if it became necessary".<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981100_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981100-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus also recognized <a href="/wiki/Pope_Victor_III" title="Pope Victor III">Pope Victor III</a> as the legitimate pope, rather than <a href="/wiki/Antipope_Clement_III" title="Antipope Clement III">Clement III</a>, who had been elected pope at Henry IV's initiative.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981100_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981100-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> However, Ladislaus provided no further support to Henry IV's opponents after he was informed of Solomon's death in 1087.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG/220px-Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="323" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG/330px-Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG/440px-Zaruke_hrvatskog_kralja_Zvonimira_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87.JPG 2x" data-file-width="817" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>The engagement of King <a href="/wiki/Demetrius_Zvonimir_of_Croatia" title="Demetrius Zvonimir of Croatia">Demetrius Zvonimir of Croatia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Helena_of_Hungary,_Queen_of_Croatia" title="Helena of Hungary, Queen of Croatia">Helen</a>, sister to Ladislaus in 1075 (painting by <a href="/wiki/Mato_Celestin_Medovi%C4%87" title="Mato Celestin Medović">Mato Celestin Medović</a>)</figcaption></figure><p>
King <a href="/wiki/Demetrius_Zvonimir_of_Croatia" title="Demetrius Zvonimir of Croatia">Demetrius Zvonimir of Croatia</a>'s wife, <a href="/wiki/Helen_of_Hungary,_Queen_of_Croatia" class="mw-redirect" title="Helen of Hungary, Queen of Croatia">Helen</a>, was Ladislaus's sister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991283_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991283-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> After the death of Zvonimir and his successor, <a href="/wiki/Stephen_II_of_Croatia" title="Stephen II of Croatia">Stephen II</a>, a conflict developed between factions of <a href="/wiki/Croatian_nobility" title="Croatian nobility">Croatian noblemen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991283_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991283-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006265_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006265-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> </p><figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg/240px-Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg" decoding="async" width="240" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg/360px-Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg/480px-Sz%C3%A9kely_Bertalan_%E2%80%93_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_kir%C3%A1ly_Horv%C3%A1torsz%C3%A1g_megh%C3%B3dit%C3%A1sa_v%C3%A9gett_%C3%A1tkel_a_Dr%C3%A1v%C3%A1n.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2336" data-file-height="1460" /></a><figcaption>King Saint Ladislaus of Hungary crosses the river <a href="/wiki/Drava" title="Drava">Drava</a> to conquer Croatia (painting by <a href="/wiki/Bertalan_Sz%C3%A9kely" title="Bertalan Székely">Bertalan Székely</a>, 19th century)</figcaption></figure><p>At Helen's request, Ladislaus intervened in the conflict and invaded Croatia in 1091.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991282,_284_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991282,_284-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The same year, he wrote to <a href="/w/index.php?title=Oderizius&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Oderizius (page does not exist)">Oderizius</a>, <a href="/wiki/Abbot_of_Monte_Cassino" class="mw-redirect" title="Abbot of Monte Cassino">Abbot of Monte Cassino</a> in Italy, about his invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006265_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006265-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Thomas_the_Archdeacon" title="Thomas the Archdeacon">Thomas the Archdeacon</a>'s chronicle describes how Ladislaus "occupied the entire land from the <a href="/wiki/River_Drava" class="mw-redirect" title="River Drava">River Drava</a> to the mountains called the Iron Alps without encountering opposition".<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012345_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012345-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748–49_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMagaš200748–49-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> However, his opponents crowned a local nobleman, <a href="/wiki/Petar_Sna%C4%8Di%C4%87" title="Petar Snačić">Petar Snačić</a>, as king.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991284_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991284-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Snačić fought in the <a href="/wiki/Mala_Kapela" title="Mala Kapela">Gvozd Mountains</a>, preventing the complete conquest of Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006265_65-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006265-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus appointed his nephew, Álmos, to administer the occupied territory.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991284_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991284-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006265_65-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006265-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Around the same time, Ladislaus set up a <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Zagreb" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Zagreb">separate diocese in Slavonia</a>, with its see in <a href="/wiki/Zagreb" title="Zagreb">Zagreb</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFine1991284_71-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFine1991284-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The bishop of the new see became the <a href="/wiki/Suffragan_bishop" title="Suffragan bishop">suffragan</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Esztergom" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Esztergom">archbishop of Esztergom</a> in Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006265_65-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006265-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ladislaus admitted in his letter to Oderizius that he could not "promote the cause of earthly dignities without committing grave sins".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Historian <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A1lint_H%C3%B3man" title="Bálint Hóman">Bálint Hóman</a> says that Ladislaus was referring to a developing conflict with <a href="/wiki/Pope_Urban_II" title="Pope Urban II">Pope Urban II</a>, who objected to Ladislaus's refusal to acknowledge the Holy See's suzerainty over Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECurta2006266_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECurta2006266-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996121–122_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996121–122-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200134_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200134-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> In the letter, Ladislaus styled himself as "king of the Hungarians and of Messia".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120_63-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006143_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006143-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMadgearu201398_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadgearu201398-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Historian Ferenc Makk writes that the latter title referred to <a href="/wiki/Moesia" title="Moesia">Moesia</a>, implying that Ladislaus had taken the regions between the <a href="/wiki/Great_Morava" title="Great Morava">Great Morava</a> and <a href="/wiki/Drina" title="Drina">Drina</a> rivers from the Byzantine Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120_63-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> No other documents refer to Ladislaus's occupation of Moesia, suggesting that if Ladislaus did occupy the region, he lost it quickly.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006143_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006143-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Alexandru Madgearu says that "Messia" should rather be associated with Bosnia, which was occupied during Ladislaus's campaign against Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMadgearu201398_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMadgearu201398-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cumans" title="Cumans">Cumans</a> invaded and plundered the eastern part of the kingdom in 1091.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120_63-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996120-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The invading Cumans were leading by chieftain Kapolcs, they broke first in <a href="/wiki/Transylvania" title="Transylvania">Transylvania</a>, then the territory between the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tisza" title="Tisza">Tisza</a> rivers. The Cumans tried to leave Hungary with their huge booty and prisoners, but King Ladislaus reached and defeated them near the <a href="/wiki/Timi%C8%99_(river)" title="Timiș (river)">Temes</a> river. Ladislaus offered the <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> for the Cuman survivors, the majority of them accepted, thus the king settled them in <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1szs%C3%A1g" title="Jászság">Jászság</a>. The rumor of the losing battle reached the Cuman camp, the Cumans threatened King Ladislaus with revenge and demanded to free the Cuman prisoners. King Ladislaus marched to the Hungarian border to prevent the next invasion. The two armies clashed near <a href="/wiki/Drobeta-Turnu_Severin" title="Drobeta-Turnu Severin">Severin</a>, the Hungarian army was victorious, King Ladislaus killed Ákos, the Cuman chieftain.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Makk argues that the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Empire" title="Byzantine Empire">Byzantines</a> persuaded them to attack Hungary,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012340_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012340-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> while the <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> states that the Cumans were incited by the "<a href="/wiki/Kievan_Rus%27" title="Kievan Rus&#39;">Ruthenians</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-Chronicon_98_138_129_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicon_98_138_129-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012339_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012339-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> In retaliation, the chronicle continues, Ladislaus invaded the neighboring <a href="/wiki/Rus%27_principalities" class="mw-redirect" title="Rus&#39; principalities">Rus' principalities</a>, forcing the "Ruthenians" to ask "for mercy" and to promise "that they would be faithful to him in all things".<sup id="cite_ref-Chronicon_98_138_129_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicon_98_138_129-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981102_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981102-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> No Rus' chronicle documents Ladislaus's military action.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981103_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981103-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bernold of St Blasien writes that Duke <a href="/wiki/Welf_I,_Duke_of_Bavaria" title="Welf I, Duke of Bavaria">Welf of Bavaria</a> prevented a conference that Emperor Henry IV "had arranged with the king of the Hungarians" in December 1092.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101_72-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981101-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> A letter written by Henry refers to "the alliance into which [he] once entered" with Ladislaus.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Pope Urban II also mentioned that the Hungarians "left the shepherds of their salvation", implying that Ladislaus had changed sides and acknowledged the legitimacy of <a href="/wiki/Antipope" title="Antipope">Antipope</a> Clement III.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006163_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMakkThoroczkay2006163-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122,_133_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122,_133-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> In the deed of the Benedictine <a href="/wiki/Somogyv%C3%A1r_Abbey" title="Somogyvár Abbey">Somogyvár Abbey</a>, Ladislaus stated that the abbot should be obedient to him, proving that Ladislaus opposed the Church's independence, which was demanded by the <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_Reform" title="Gregorian Reform">Gregorian Reforms</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996116_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996116-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus personally presided over <a href="/wiki/Synod_of_Szabolcs" title="Synod of Szabolcs">an assembly of the Hungarian prelates</a> that met in <a href="/wiki/Szabolcs_(village)" title="Szabolcs (village)">Szabolcs</a> on 21 May 1091.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981105_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981105-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> The synod recognized the legitimacy of a clergyman's first marriage, in contrast to the <a href="/wiki/Clerical_celibacy_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Clerical celibacy (Catholic Church)">requirements of canon law</a>, which states that members of the clergy may not marry at all.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981108_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981108-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> According to a scholarly theory, the sees of the dioceses of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Kalocsa-Kecskem%C3%A9t" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Kalocsa-Kecskemét">Kalocsa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Oradea_Mare" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Oradea Mare">Bihar</a> were moved to <a href="/wiki/Ba%C4%8D,_Serbia" title="Bač, Serbia">Bács</a> (now Bač, <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Nagyv%C3%A1rad" class="mw-redirect" title="Nagyvárad">Nagyvárad</a> (present-day Oradea, Romania), respectively, during Ladislaus's reign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996117-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel200143_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel200143-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Last_years_.281092.E2.80.931095.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Last_years_(1092–1095)">Last years (1092–1095)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Last years (1092–1095)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hungary_11th_cent.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="11th-century Hungary" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Hungary_11th_cent.png/220px-Hungary_11th_cent.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Hungary_11th_cent.png/330px-Hungary_11th_cent.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Hungary_11th_cent.png/440px-Hungary_11th_cent.png 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2078" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a> in the 1090s</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ladislaus intervened in a conflict between <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_I_Herman" title="Władysław I Herman">Władysław I Herman</a>, <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Poland" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Poland">Duke of Poland</a>, and the duke's illegitimate son, <a href="/wiki/Zbigniew_of_Poland" title="Zbigniew of Poland">Zbigniew</a>, on the latter's behalf.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> He marched to Poland and captured Herman's younger son, <a href="/wiki/Boles%C5%82aw_III_Wrymouth" title="Bolesław III Wrymouth">Boleslaus</a>, in 1093.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101_94-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> At Ladislaus's demand, Władysław I Herman declared Zbigniew his legitimate son.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101–102_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEManteuffel1982101–102-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> also mentions that the Hungarian troops captured <a href="/wiki/Cracow" class="mw-redirect" title="Cracow">Cracow</a> during Ladislaus's campaign, but the credibility of this report has been questioned.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBárány2012340_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBárány2012340-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> states that "messengers from <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> and from <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>, from <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a> and <a href="/wiki/Great_Britain" title="Great Britain">Britain</a>, and especially from Willermus, the brother of the King of the Franks" visited Ladislaus in Bodrog (near present-day <a href="/wiki/Ba%C4%8Dki_Mono%C5%A1tor" title="Bački Monoštor">Bački Monoštor</a> in Serbia) on Easter 1095, asking him to lead their <a href="/wiki/Crusade" class="mw-redirect" title="Crusade">crusade</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981104_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKosztolnyik1981104-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus's legend says that he decided "to go to <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a>, and to die there for Christ".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002186_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002186-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The whole story was  invented, probably during the reign of King <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_III_of_Hungary" title="Béla III of Hungary">Béla III of Hungary</a> (who was actually planning to lead a crusade to the Holy Land in the 1190s), according to historian Gábor Klaniczay.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002418_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002418-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> However, Ladislaus did plan to invade Bohemia, because he wanted to assist <a href="/wiki/Euphemia_of_Hungary" title="Euphemia of Hungary">his sister</a>'s sons, <a href="/wiki/Svatopluk,_Duke_of_Bohemia" title="Svatopluk, Duke of Bohemia">Svatopluk</a> and <a href="/wiki/Otto_II_the_Black" title="Otto II the Black">Otto</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> He became seriously ill before reaching Moravia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFont200115_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFont200115-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The <i>Illuminated Chronicle</i> narrates that Ladislaus, who had no sons, "called together his chief men", telling them that his brother's younger son, Álmos, "should reign after him".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFont200115_100-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFont200115-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ladislaus died near the Hungarian-Bohemian border on 29 July 1095.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122_81-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996122-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">papal bull</a> of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paschal_II" title="Pope Paschal II">Pope Paschal II</a> in 1106 states that Ladislaus's "venerable body rests" in Somogyvár Abbey, implying that Ladislaus had been buried in <a href="/wiki/Somogyv%C3%A1r" title="Somogyvár">Somogyvár</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002175_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002175-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> On the other hand, Ladislaus's late 12th-century "Legend" provides that his attendants buried him in <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kesfeh%C3%A9rv%C3%A1r" title="Székesfehérvár">Székesfehérvár</a>, but the cart carrying his body "set out to <a href="/wiki/Nagyv%C3%A1rad" class="mw-redirect" title="Nagyvárad">Várad</a> on its own, unassisted by any draft animal".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002175_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlaniczay2002175-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_(cropped1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg/220px-Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="326" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg/330px-Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg/440px-Mosaic_of_Irene_of_Hungary_%28cropped1%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="887" data-file-height="1314" /></a><figcaption>Mosaic portrait of Ladislaus's daughter, <a href="/wiki/Irene_of_Hungary" title="Irene of Hungary">Piroska</a>, who was known as Empress Irene in the Byzantine Empire, in the <a href="/wiki/Hagia_Sophia" title="Hagia Sophia">Hagia Sophia</a> (<a href="/wiki/Istanbul" title="Istanbul">Istanbul</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a>). She is venerated as Saint Irene by the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Historian Gyula Kristó says that Ladislaus had a first wife,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> but her name and family are not known.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> She gave birth to a daughter, whose name is also unknown.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus's daughter married Prince <a href="/wiki/Iaroslav_Sviatopolkovich" title="Iaroslav Sviatopolkovich">Iaroslav Sviatopolchich of Volhinia</a> around 1090.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Ladislaus married again in 1078, to <a href="/wiki/Adelaide_of_Rheinfelden" title="Adelaide of Rheinfelden">Adelaide</a>, a daughter of the German <a href="/wiki/Anti-king" title="Anti-king">anti-king</a> <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_of_Rheinfelden" title="Rudolf of Rheinfelden">Rudolf of Swabia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118_49-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996118-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Their only known child, <a href="/wiki/Irene_of_Hungary" title="Irene of Hungary">Piroska</a>, became the wife of the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Emperor" class="mw-redirect" title="Byzantine Emperor">Byzantine Emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/John_II_Komnenos" title="John II Komnenos">John II Komnenos</a> in 1105 or 1106.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996Appendix_2_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996Appendix_2-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ladislaus's family and relatives who are mentioned in the article are shown in the following family tree.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996Appendices_1–2_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKristóMakk1996Appendices_1–2-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
